```text
1	---
2	dream_id: dream_20260517T075852

6	---
7	
8	# Dream Review Report: "Characterizing Recursive Generation Depth: Feature-Based Estimation, Degradation Atlas, and Boundary Conditions"
9	
10	**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 Main  
11	**Review mode**: Full (2 Reviewers, 4-step roundtable)  
12	**Scoring basis**: ACL Rolling Review (ARR) form, 1-5 scale with half-point increments
13	
14	---
15	
16	## Step 1: Independent Reviews
17	
18	### Reviewer 1 (R1) — Focus: Methodology + Experiments
19	
20	**Summary**: The paper formalizes ordinal generation depth estimation as a 4-class classification task, constructs a 3-model x 3-strategy x 4-depth benchmark, and trains RF classifiers on 19 Pythia-160M-derived statistical features, achieving 70-85% accuracy under controlled conditions. The primary contribution is the Depth Degradation Atlas, which reveals perplexity tail statistic universality, non-uniform inter-depth distances (formalized as EDD), and strategy-dependent trajectory reversal. Section 5 systematically characterizes three boundary conditions (scale, generation mode, domain shift) under which the signal attenuates.
21	
22	**Strengths**:
23	- Well-defined problem formalization with clear distinction from binary AI-text detection. The ordinal structure is properly motivated by the data curation use case.
24	- Thorough 3x3 experimental atlas with systematic evaluation across all model-strategy combinations. Table 1 is informative and well-structured.
25	- Feature universality finding is robust: p90_ppl and var_surprisal consistently dominate across all 9 cells, connecting to Dohmatob et al.'s (2024) tail-erosion theory.
26	- Unusually honest reporting of negative results (DCOL failure, cross-strategy breakdown, inconclusive curation implications) and boundary conditions. The paper does not overclaim.
27	- EDD metric is well-defined (Equation 1) and provides a useful framework for quantifying discriminability limits.
28	- Boundary condition characterization (Section 5) is comprehensive: three dimensions (scale, generation mode, domain) each with dedicated experiments, tables, and mechanistic explanations.
29	
30	**Weaknesses (Major)**:
31	- **M1: No statistical significance testing on main results (Table 1).** The 9-cell atlas reports point estimates without confidence intervals. Are the inter-cell differences (e.g., Pythia top-k 70.3% vs GPT-2 XL temp 85.4%) statistically significant? The 5-fold CV provides fold-level estimates that could yield CIs, but none are reported. This is a notable gap for an empirical characterization paper.
32	- **M2: No comparison with existing AI-text detectors adapted for ordinal depth.** Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT produce continuous perplexity-based scores that could be calibrated or thresholded for ordinal depth estimation. Not comparing against these obvious baselines leaves the contribution poorly contextualized. The paper only mentions zero-shot LLMs in passing but does not present formal baseline comparisons.
33	- **M3: d0 separability inflates headline metrics.** d0v1 AUC > 0.99 across all settings means d0 recall > 98%, trivially inflating the 70-85% four-class accuracy. The practically relevant metric is d1-d3 three-class accuracy. The paper reports this in some tables but the Abstract and Introduction lead with the inflated 4-class number.
34	- **M4: Reference model dependency is underexplored.** All experiments use Pythia-160M as the sole reference model. The cross-scale perplexity measurement gap (Section 5.1) is correctly identified as the bottleneck for 14B, but no alternative reference models are tested at scale (e.g., using a 7B reference for 14B outputs). This leaves open whether the boundary conditions are fundamental or artifacts of the Pythia-160M choice.
35	
36	**Weaknesses (Minor)**:
37	- Sample size justification is missing. Why 1,000 passages per condition? No power analysis or convergence curve is provided.
38	- The 19 features are standard in AI-text detection literature. No exploration of whether additional features (e.g., intrinsic dimension per Tulchinskii et al. 2024) could improve performance.
39	- LoRA target modules are not specified. Different target modules may produce different depth signals.
40	
41	**R1 Scores**: Overall 3.0, Soundness 3.0, Excitement 3.0, Confidence 4, Reproducibility 3.5
42	
43	---
44	
45	### Reviewer 2 (R2) — Focus: Novelty + Related Work
46	
47	**Summary**: The paper applies Random Forest classification on 19 perplexity-derived statistical features to estimate how many recursive fine-tuning cycles text has undergone. The core method is standard (RF on known features); the contribution rests on the task formalization, systematic experimentation across a controlled grid, and the boundary condition analysis showing where the approach fails.
48	
49	**Strengths**:
50	- Clear task formalization with practical motivation. The distinction between shallow and deep recursive text for data curation is well-argued.
51	- The strategy-dependent trajectory reversal (Section 4.4) is the most novel empirical finding. Temperature sampling causing d1v2 AUC to collapse to 0.13 in cross-strategy LOO is genuinely surprising and has both scientific and practical implications.
52	- Boundary condition framing (Section 5) is intellectually honest. Characterizing where a method fails is valuable, especially when the failures are systematic and mechanistically explained.
53	- Negative DCOL result (Section 4.5) is informative for the community, showing that end-to-end learning faces inherent difficulties on this task.
54	
55	**Weaknesses (Major)**:
56	- **M1: Limited novelty beyond systematic experimentation.** The core method is RF on perplexity statistics, both of which are standard tools. The finding that perplexity tails capture depth is predicted by existing model collapse theory (Dohmatob et al. 2024). The "Atlas" is descriptive rather than predictive and does not transfer to new models/strategies without re-running the full pipeline.
57	- **M2: Ecological validity gap is fundamental.** Under all three boundary conditions tested, EDD collapses from 3 to 1, meaning ordinal depth estimation degrades to binary real-vs-synthetic detection. The practical framing (data curation, early warning) is undermined by: (a) 50% accuracy under prompted generation, (b) 55% at 14B, (c) 23-30% FPR on web-crawl. In realistic scenarios, multiple boundary conditions would compound simultaneously.
58	- **M3: Cross-strategy transfer is broken for practical use.** Cross-strategy LOO d1-d3 accuracy of ~46% (chance 33%) and the d1v2 AUC inversion (0.13 for GPT-2 XL temp) mean the method requires knowing the decoding strategy a priori. The two-stage pipeline in Discussion (Table 10) confirms this: predicted two-stage (70.6%) underperforms naive pooled (72.0%) because strategy classification at 57.5% is insufficient.
59	- **M4: Scale degradation raises questions about future relevance.** At 14B, accuracy drops to 55% with EDD=1. As frontier models grow to 70B+, the Pythia-160M reference model's perplexity landscape may become entirely uninformative. The paper does not address whether this approach has a viable scaling path.
60	
61	**Weaknesses (Minor)**:
62	- ~22 references is thin for EMNLP. Missing important related work: multi-class provenance estimation, large-scale data filtering pipelines (FineWeb, DataComp), distribution testing methods (MMD, KL divergence).
63	- The curation PoC mentioned in Discussion is inconclusive, weakening the practical motivation.
64	- Discussion section is compressed. The cross-strategy pipeline results deserve fuller treatment.
65	
66	**R2 Scores**: Overall 2.5, Soundness 3.5, Excitement 2.0, Confidence 4, Reproducibility 4.0
67	
68	---
69	
70	## Step 2: Cross-Sharing
71	
72	**R1 findings shared with R2**:
73	- R1 notes the absence of CIs on Table 1, which R2 had not flagged. R2 agrees this is a gap but considers it minor relative to the novelty concerns.
74	- R1's concern about reference model dependency at scale resonates with R2's point about future relevance. Both agree this is a structural limitation.
75	
76	**R2 findings shared with R1**:
77	- R2's argument that the main finding (perplexity tails capture depth) is predicted by existing theory (Dohmatob et al. 2024) is accepted by R1 as reducing the novelty of the Atlas. R1 adjusts: the Atlas's value is more in the systematic measurement than in surprising discoveries.
78	- R2's point about missing calibrated detector baselines (Binoculars, Fast-DetectGPT) is strongly endorsed by R1. Both agree this is a significant experimental omission.
79	- R2's framing of the ecological validity problem as compound (multiple boundary conditions would apply simultaneously in practice) sharpens R1's concern.
80	
81	---
82	
83	## Step 3: Convergence
84	
85	**Agreed strengths**:
86	1. Task formalization is novel and well-motivated (both reviewers)
87	2. Experimental thoroughness is commendable (both reviewers)
88	3. Honest limitation reporting builds credibility (both reviewers)
89	4. Strategy-dependent trajectory reversal is the most novel finding (both reviewers)
90	5. Feature universality (p90_ppl, var_surprisal) is a genuine empirical contribution (both reviewers)
91	
92	**Agreed weaknesses**:
93	1. Missing calibrated detector baselines (Binoculars, Fast-DetectGPT adapted for ordinal depth). This is the most significant experimental gap.
94	2. d0 inflation of headline metrics. The 70-85% number is misleading; d1-d3 discrimination is the hard problem.
95	3. Ecological validity gap is fundamental. All three boundary conditions collapse EDD to 1, and real-world scenarios would compound these conditions.
96	4. Cross-strategy transfer is broken and the two-stage pipeline does not help (predicted < naive pooled).
97	5. Reference model (Pythia-160M) dependency limits both scale and practical applicability.
98	
99	**Disagreement resolved**:
100	- R1 initially scored Soundness 3.0 vs R2's 3.5. After discussion, both converge to **3.0**: the experiments are thorough within their scope, but the missing baselines and absent CIs on the main table prevent a "strong" soundness rating.
101	- R1 scored Excitement 3.0 vs R2's 2.0. After R2's argument that the core findings are predicted by existing theory and the practical applicability is severely constrained, R1 adjusts to **2.5**. R2 adjusts slightly upward to **2.5**, acknowledging the strategy-dependent trajectory finding has genuine scientific value. Converged: **2.5**.
102	
103	---
104	
105	## Step 4: Consolidated Assessment
106	
107	### Paper Strengths (ranked by importance)
108	
109	1. **Novel task formalization**: Ordinal generation depth estimation is a genuine gap between binary AI-text detection and model collapse characterization. The problem is well-defined, practically motivated, and no prior work addresses it directly.
110	
111	2. **Strategy-dependent trajectory reversal**: The finding that temperature sampling causes feature-boundary reversal at the d1/d2 interface (cross-strategy LOO d1v2 AUC = 0.13) is scientifically interesting and practically important. The mechanistic explanation (uniform logit scaling accelerates convergence to attractor distribution) is convincing.
112	
113	3. **Honest boundary characterization**: Section 5 systematically maps where the method fails. This is unusually transparent for a submission and provides genuine value by delineating the scope of feature-based approaches.
114	
115	4. **Feature universality**: p90_ppl and var_surprisal consistently dominate feature importance across all 9 model-strategy cells, connecting empirical findings to theoretical predictions about tail erosion in recursive generation.
116	
117	5. **EDD metric**: A clean formalization of discriminability limits that could be reused in future work on recursive generation analysis.
118	
119	### Paper Weaknesses (ranked by severity)
120	
121	1. **[Critical] Missing calibrated detector baselines**: Existing perplexity-based detectors (Binoculars, Fast-DetectGPT) produce continuous scores that could be directly calibrated for ordinal depth estimation. Not comparing against these baselines makes it impossible to assess whether the 19-feature RF approach offers any advantage over simpler existing methods adapted to the ordinal setting. This is the most significant experimental gap.
122	
123	2. **[Major] Ecological validity collapses under realistic conditions**: Under each of three boundary conditions (14B scale, prompted generation, web-crawl domain), EDD collapses from 3 to 1, reducing ordinal depth estimation to binary real-vs-synthetic detection. In practice, multiple conditions would compound. The practical framing (data curation) is therefore unsupported by the evidence.
124	
125	3. **[Major] Limited methodological novelty**: The method (RF on perplexity summary statistics) uses entirely standard tools. The primary finding (perplexity tails capture depth) follows from existing model collapse theory. The "Atlas" is a systematic experiment, not a reusable methodology or predictive framework.
126	
127	4. **[Major] Cross-strategy transfer is fundamentally broken**: The d1v2 AUC inversion and the failure of the two-stage pipeline (predicted 70.6% < naive pooled 72.0%) mean the method is unusable without prior knowledge of the decoding strategy. This severely limits the practical contribution.
128	
129	5. **[Moderate] d0 inflation of headline metrics**: The Abstract's "70 to 85% four-class accuracy" is inflated by near-perfect d0 detection. The d1-d3 three-class problem, which is the practically relevant task, achieves substantially lower accuracy.
130	
131	6. **[Moderate] No confidence intervals on main results (Table 1)**: An empirical characterization paper should report uncertainty on its primary measurements. The 5-fold CV structure permits bootstrap CIs, but none are provided.
132	
133	7. **[Minor] Thin reference list (~22 citations)**: Missing coverage of multi-class provenance detection, large-scale data filtering pipelines, and distribution testing methods. Below typical EMNLP density.
134	
135	8. **[Minor] Single reference model (Pythia-160M)**: No alternative reference models tested at scale. The cross-scale perplexity gap may be solvable with scale-matched references, but this is untested.
136	
137	### Questions for Authors
138	
139	1. **Calibrated detector baselines**: Can Binoculars or Fast-DetectGPT scores be calibrated for ordinal depth estimation? If so, how does RF on 19 features compare? If not, why?
140	
141	2. **Compound boundary conditions**: What happens when multiple boundary conditions apply simultaneously (e.g., prompted generation from a 14B model on web-crawl data)? The paper tests each dimension independently but real scenarios compound them.
142	
143	3. **Scale path**: Given that Pythia-160M's perplexity landscape compresses the depth gradient at 14B, have you considered scale-matched reference models (e.g., 7B reference for 14B outputs)? Is there a principled way to select reference models?
144	
145	4. **d0 reframing**: Would you consider reporting d1-d3 three-class accuracy as the primary metric, given that d0 detection is trivial?
146	
147	5. **Cross-strategy practical guidance**: Given the pipeline failure, what is the practical recommendation for a data curator who does not know the decoding strategy used? Is the method usable in any realistic deployment scenario?
148	
149	### Experiment Coverage Assessment
150	
151	Comparing against the full experiment design (review_context.yaml):
152	
153	| Experiment | Status | Paper Coverage |
154	|---|---|---|
155	| DCOL ablation (plan_010) | Cancelled (DCOL failed) | Section 4.5 covers DCOL failure adequately |
156	| 13B FP16 validation (plan_014) | Expected 55% acc, EDD=1 | Section 5.1, Table 3: 55% acc, EDD=1. Fully covered. |
157	| Wild-data calibration (plan_015) | Expected 23-30% FPR | Section 5.3, Table 8: 23-30% FPR. Fully covered. |
158	| Prompted generation (plan_016) | Expected 50% acc, EDD=1 | Section 5.2, Tables 5-7: 50% acc, EDD=1. Fully covered. |
159	| Cross-strategy pipeline (plan_017) | Expected 57.5% clf, predicted < naive | Discussion + Table 10: 57.5% clf, 70.6% < 72.0%. Covered. |
160	
161	All planned experiments are present in the paper. Coverage is complete.
162	
163	### Consolidated Scores
164	
165	| Dimension | R1 | R2 | Consolidated | Justification |
166	|---|---|---|---|---|
167	| **Overall** | 3.0 | 2.5 | **2.5** | Borderline Findings / resubmit. The task formalization and experimental thoroughness support a Findings-level contribution, but the missing baselines, limited novelty, and collapsed ecological validity prevent a confident Findings recommendation. |
168	| **Soundness** | 3.0 | 3.5 | **3.0** | Acceptable. Experiments are thorough within scope, but missing CIs on main results and absent calibrated detector baselines are significant gaps for an empirical paper. |
169	| **Excitement** | 3.0 | 2.0 | **2.5** | Borderline interesting. The strategy-dependent trajectory finding is genuinely novel, but the core method is standard, the main finding is predicted by theory, and practical applicability is severely limited. |
170	| **Confidence** | 4.0 | 4.0 | **4.0** | Both reviewers are confident in their assessment. The paper's scope is well within reviewers' expertise. |
171	| **Reproducibility** | 3.5 | 4.0 | **3.5** | Mostly reproducible. Hyperparameters are specified, but some implementation details (LoRA targets, Wikipedia subset, exact feature computation) need clarification. Code release would resolve this. |
172	
173	### Recommendation
174	
175	**Borderline Findings / Resubmit** (Overall 2.5). The paper addresses a genuinely novel task with commendable experimental thoroughness and intellectual honesty. However, three issues prevent acceptance: (1) no comparison with existing detectors adapted for ordinal depth, leaving the contribution's magnitude unclear; (2) the method uses entirely standard tools (RF on perplexity statistics) with findings largely predicted by existing model collapse theory; (3) ecological validity collapses under all tested realistic conditions, with EDD universally dropping to 1. The strategy-dependent trajectory reversal is the strongest finding and could anchor a more focused paper. For resubmission, we recommend: (a) adding calibrated Binoculars/Fast-DetectGPT baselines, (b) providing CIs on all main results, (c) either demonstrating a viable scaling path (scale-matched reference models) or reframing the contribution as purely diagnostic/scientific rather than practically applicable.
176

The primary contribution is the Depth Degradation Atlas, which reveals perplexity tail statistic universality, non-uniform inter-depth distances (formalized as EDD), and strategy-dependent trajectory reversal. Section 5 systematically characterizes three boundary conditions (scale, generation mode, domain shift) under which the signal attenuates.\n\n**Strengths**:\n- Well-defined problem formalization with clear distinction from binary AI-text detection. The ordinal structure is properly motivated by the data curation use case.\n- Thorough 3x3 experimental atlas with systematic evaluation across all model-strategy combinations. Table 1 is informative and well-structured.\n- Feature universality finding is robust: p90_ppl and var_surprisal consistently dominate across all 9 cells, connecting to Dohmatob et al.'s (2024) tail-erosion theory.\n- Unusually honest reporting of negative results (DCOL failure, cross-strategy breakdown, inconclusive curation implications) and boundary conditions. The paper does not overclaim.\n- EDD metric is well-defined (Equation 1) and provides a useful framework for quantifying discriminability limits.\n- Boundary condition characterization (Section 5) is comprehensive: three dimensions (scale, generation mode, domain) each with dedicated experiments, tables, and mechanistic explanations.\n\n**Weaknesses (Major)**:\n- **M1: No statistical significance testing on main results (Table 1).** The 9-cell atlas reports point estimates without confidence intervals. Are the inter-cell differences (e.g., Pythia top-k 70.3% vs GPT-2 XL temp 85.4%) statistically significant? The 5-fold CV provides fold-level estimates that could yield CIs, but none are reported. This is a notable gap for an empirical characterization paper.\n- **M2: No comparison with existing AI-text detectors adapted for ordinal depth.** Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT produce continuous perplexity-based scores that could be calibrated or thresholded for ordinal depth estimation. Not comparing against these obvious baselines leaves the contribution poorly contextualized. The paper only mentions zero-shot LLMs in passing but does not present formal baseline comparisons.\n- **M3: d0 separability inflates headline metrics.** d0v1 AUC > 0.99 across all settings means d0 recall > 98%, trivially inflating the 70-85% four-class accuracy. The practically relevant metric is d1-d3 three-class accuracy. The paper reports this in some tables but the Abstract and Introduction lead with the inflated 4-class number.\n- **M4: Reference model dependency is underexplored.** All experiments use Pythia-160M as the sole reference model. The cross-scale perplexity measurement gap (Section 5.1) is correctly identified as the bottleneck for 14B, but no alternative reference models are tested at scale (e.g., using a 7B reference for 14B outputs). This leaves open whether the boundary conditions are fundamental or artifacts of the Pythia-160M choice.\n\n**Weaknesses (Minor)**:\n- Sample size justification is missing. Why 1,000 passages per condition? No power analysis or convergence curve is provided.\n- The 19 features are standard in AI-text detection literature. No exploration of whether additional features (e.g., intrinsic dimension per Tulchinskii et al. 2024) could improve performance.\n- LoRA target modules are not specified. Different target modules may produce different depth signals.\n\n**R1 Scores**: Overall 3.0, Soundness 3.0, Excitement 3.0, Confidence 4, Reproducibility 3.5\n\n---\n\n### Reviewer 2 (R2) — Focus: Novelty + Related Work\n\n**Summary**: The paper applies Random Forest classification on 19 perplexity-derived statistical features to estimate how many recursive fine-tuning cycles text has undergone. The core method is standard (RF on known features); the contribution rests on the task formalization, systematic experimentation across a controlled grid, and the boundary condition analysis showing where the approach fails.\n\n**Strengths**:\n- Clear task formalization with practical motivation. The distinction between shallow and deep recursive text for data curation is well-argued.\n- The strategy-dependent trajectory reversal (Section 4.4) is the most novel empirical finding. Temperature sampling causing d1v2 AUC to collapse to 0.13 in cross-strategy LOO is genuinely surprising and has both scientific and practical implications.\n- Boundary condition framing (Section 5) is intellectually honest. Characterizing where a method fails is valuable, especially when the failures are systematic and mechanistically explained.\n- Negative DCOL result (Section 4.5) is informative for the community, showing that end-to-end learning faces inherent difficulties on this task.\n\n**Weaknesses (Major)**:\n- **M1: Limited novelty beyond systematic experimentation.** The core method is RF on perplexity statistics, both of which are standard tools. The finding that perplexity tails capture depth is predicted by existing model collapse theory (Dohmatob et al. 2024). The \"Atlas\" is descriptive rather than predictive and does not transfer to new models/strategies without re-running the full pipeline.\n- **M2: Ecological validity gap is fundamental.** Under all three boundary conditions tested, EDD collapses from 3 to 1, meaning ordinal depth estimation degrades to binary real-vs-synthetic detection. The practical framing (data curation, early warning) is undermined by: (a) 50% accuracy under prompted generation, (b) 55% at 14B, (c) 23-30% FPR on web-crawl. In realistic scenarios, multiple boundary conditions would compound simultaneously.\n- **M3: Cross-strategy transfer is broken for practical use.** Cross-strategy LOO d1-d3 accuracy of ~46% (chance 33%) and the d1v2 AUC inversion (0.13 for GPT-2 XL temp) mean the method requires knowing the decoding strategy a priori. The two-stage pipeline in Discussion (Table 10) confirms this: predicted two-stage (70.6%) underperforms naive pooled (72.0%) because strategy classification at 57.5% is insufficient.\n- **M4: Scale degradation raises questions about future relevance.** At 14B, accuracy drops to 55% with EDD=1. As frontier models grow to 70B+, the Pythia-160M reference model's perplexity landscape may become entirely uninformative. The paper does not address whether this approach has a viable scaling path.\n\n**Weaknesses (Minor)**:\n- ~22 references is thin for EMNLP. Missing important related work: multi-class provenance estimation, large-scale data filtering pipelines (FineWeb, DataComp), distribution testing methods (MMD, KL divergence).\n- The curation PoC mentioned in Discussion is inconclusive, weakening the practical motivation.\n- Discussion section is compressed. R2 agrees this is a gap but considers it minor relative to the novelty concerns.\n- R1's concern about reference model dependency at scale resonates with R2's point about future relevance. Both agree this is a structural limitation.\n\n**R2 findings shared with R1**:\n- R2's argument that the main finding (perplexity tails capture depth) is predicted by existing theory (Dohmatob et al. 2024) is accepted by R1 as reducing the novelty of the Atlas. Missing calibrated detector baselines (Binoculars, Fast-DetectGPT adapted for ordinal depth). This is the most significant experimental gap.\n2. d0 inflation of headline metrics. The 70-85% number is misleading; d1-d3 discrimination is the hard problem.\n3. Ecological validity gap is fundamental. All three boundary conditions collapse EDD to 1, and real-world scenarios would compound these conditions.\n4. After discussion, both converge to **3.0**: the experiments are thorough within their scope, but the missing baselines and absent CIs on the main table prevent a \"strong\" soundness rating.\n- R1 scored Excitement 3.0 vs R2's 2.0. After R2's argument that the core findings are predicted by existing theory and the practical applicability is severely constrained, R1 adjusts to **2.5**. R2 adjusts slightly upward to **2.5**, acknowledging the strategy-dependent trajectory finding has genuine scientific value. Converged: **2.5**.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Consolidated Assessment\n\n### Paper Strengths (ranked by importance)\n\n1. **Novel task formalization**: Ordinal generation depth estimation is a genuine gap between binary AI-text detection and model collapse characterization. The problem is well-defined, practically motivated, and no prior work addresses it directly.\n\n2. **Strategy-dependent trajectory reversal**: The finding that temperature sampling causes feature-boundary reversal at the d1/d2 interface (cross-strategy LOO d1v2 AUC = 0.13) is scientifically interesting and practically important. The mechanistic explanation (uniform logit scaling accelerates convergence to attractor distribution) is convincing.\n\n3. **Honest boundary characterization**: Section 5 systematically maps where the method fails. This is unusually transparent for a submission and provides genuine value by delineating the scope of feature-based approaches.\n\n4. **Feature universality**: p90_ppl and var_surprisal consistently dominate feature importance across all 9 model-strategy cells, connecting empirical findings to theoretical predictions about tail erosion in recursive generation.\n\n5. **EDD metric**: A clean formalization of discriminability limits that could be reused in future work on recursive generation analysis.\n\n### Paper Weaknesses (ranked by severity)\n\n1. **[Critical] Missing calibrated detector baselines**: Existing perplexity-based detectors (Binoculars, Fast-DetectGPT) produce continuous scores that could be directly calibrated for ordinal depth estimation. Not comparing against these baselines makes it impossible to assess whether the 19-feature RF approach offers any advantage over simpler existing methods adapted to the ordinal setting. This is the most significant experimental gap.\n\n2. **[Major] Ecological validity collapses under realistic conditions**: Under each of three boundary conditions (14B scale, prompted generation, web-crawl domain), EDD collapses from 3 to 1, reducing ordinal depth estimation to binary real-vs-synthetic detection. The practical framing (data curation) is therefore unsupported by the evidence.\n\n3. **[Major] Limited methodological novelty**: The method (RF on perplexity summary statistics) uses entirely standard tools. 8	# Dream Review Report — Ordinal Depth Estimation for Recursive LM Generations
9	
10	**Venue:** EMNLP 2026 Main (ACL Rolling Review)
11	**Paper:** "Ordinal Depth Estimation for Recursive Language Model Generations: A Depth Degradation Atlas Across Scales and Strategies"
12	**Reviewers:** R1 (Method & Experiments), R2 (Novelty & Related Work)
13	
14	---
15	
16	## Scores (ARR 1–5 scale)
17	
18	| Dimension | R1 | R2 | Final |
19	|-----------|----|----|-------|
20	| Soundness | 4 | 4 | **4** |
21	| Excitement | 3 | 3 | **3** |
22	| Overall Assessment | 3 | 3 | **3** |
23	
24	**Disposition:** Borderline accept for EMNLP Main. Strong candidate for Findings.
25	
26	---
27	
28	## Experiment Coverage vs. Imagined Experiments
29	
30	| Imagined Experiment | Plan ID | Paper Section | Status |
31	|---------------------|---------|---------------|--------|
32	| 13B Clean FP16 Scale Validation | plan_014 | Section 5.2, Table 2 | Fully covered |
33	| Prompted Generation Ecological Validity | plan_016 | Section 5.3, Tables 3-4 | Fully covered |
34	| Cross-Strategy Two-Stage Pipeline (Improved) | plan_017 | Section 5.4, Tables 5-6 | Fully covered |
35	| Wild-Data Calibration on RedPajama/Dolma | plan_015 | Section 5.5, Table 7 | Fully covered |
36	
37	**Coverage: 4/4 (100%).** All imagined experiments are present in the paper with results matching expected ranges.
38	
39	---
40	
41	## R1 Review: Method & Experiments
42	
43	### Strengths
44	
45	1. **Clean task formalization.** The ordinal depth estimation problem is precisely defined (Eq. 1-2). The recursive generation chain formulation is unambiguous and creates a clear experimental protocol. The EDD metric (Eq. 2) naturally captures the "depth horizon" of discriminability and provides a useful single-number summary across configurations.
46	
47	2. **Comprehensive benchmark design.** The 3-model x 3-strategy x 4-depth grid (9 configurations, 36 cells) is thorough for a first study. The choice of diverse model families (Pythia, OLMo, GPT-2 XL) with different architectures and training data strengthens generalizability claims. Extension to d=5 for limit analysis is appropriate.
48	
49	3. **Fixed reference model design.** Using Pythia-1.4B as a fixed reference across all experiments is a key methodological insight. It ensures features reflect text properties rather than generator-reference alignment, and enables cross-model comparability. The 14B experiment validates this: Pythia-1.4B retains discriminative power on 14B-generated text with only 2-4pp degradation.
50	
51	4. **Feature interpretability.** 19 features in 4 families (perplexity, surprisal, diversity, fluency) are transparent, reproducible, and computationally cheap. The finding that p90_ppl and var_surprisal are universal top-2 features across all 9 configurations, at 14B scale, and under prompted generation is a robust empirical result.
52	
53	5. **Scale validation addresses the right concern.** Using Qwen2.5-14B in clean FP16 (no quantization) directly resolves the 7B QLoRA confound (U-shaped curves). The result (76.2%, within 1B range) falsifies the "small model artifact" hypothesis. Identifying the U-shape as quantization noise is a useful methodological finding.
54	
55	6. **Ecological validity is statistically supported.** The prompted generation experiment (50 prompts, 5 domain clusters, 92.8% accuracy retention) with KS tests confirming feature distribution stability (p > 0.05 for all top-5 features) provides solid evidence. Cross-topic variance is small (std = 3.1pp), ruling out content domain as a confound.
56	
57	7. **Two-stage pipeline shows systematic problem-solving.** The v1 (57.5% strategy clf) to v2 (81.2%) progression with principled feature additions targeting strategy-discriminative signals is well-motivated. The oracle analysis (78.4% ≈ within-strategy 78.2%, gap = 0.2pp) confirms the architecture ceiling and localizes remaining error to strategy misclassification.
58	
59	8. **Honest limitations.** Five specific limitations clearly described. Wild-data temporal analysis appropriately caveated ("does not constitute causal evidence"). Reference model circularity discussed with two mitigating observations.
60	
61	### Weaknesses
62	
63	1. **No neural classifier baselines.** Only RF (500 trees) and LR are evaluated. Even a simple MLP on the 19 features, or a fine-tuned probe (BERT/DeBERTa on raw text), would contextualize whether hand-crafted features capture the full depth signal. The machine-generated text detection field has moved toward neural methods; ignoring them entirely is a gap. This is particularly important because the low d2 recall (26.8-63%) suggests current features may be insufficient for middle depths.
64	
65	2. **Sample sizes not clearly reported.** The paper never explicitly states how many passages are generated per depth per model-strategy configuration. For 5-fold stratified CV, total N per class directly affects CI width and the reliability of the reported accuracies. This is a significant reproducibility concern.
66	
67	3. **d2 recall is problematically low.** Per-class recall for d2 ranges from 26.8% to 63.0% across configurations. The 14B confusion matrix (Figure 3) shows d2 recall of 0.58 with 25% of d2 classified as d3. For practical data curation, graduated decisions at middle depths are exactly the advertised use case. This undermines the practical contribution.
68	
69	4. **Single 14B data point for "scale robustness."** The claim that the signal persists "at the 14B scale" rests on one experiment with one model (Qwen2.5-14B) and one strategy (nucleus). No 30B+ or 70B+ experiments. A single supporting data point does not establish "scale robustness" as a general finding.
70	
71	5. **LoRA-only fine-tuning.** All recursive chains use LoRA. Full fine-tuning, continued pretraining, or other adaptation methods (prefix tuning, adapters) could produce different depth signatures. The paper doesn't discuss whether LoRA's low-rank constraint specifically shapes the distributional shift pattern.
72	
73	6. **No held-out test set.** 5-fold stratified CV means every data point participates in training at some point. A proper train/dev/test split would provide stronger accuracy claims, especially for the headline numbers.
74	
75	7. **Wild-data FPR estimation is assumption-dependent.** The FPR estimates assume web corpora are "predominantly human-written," but without ground-truth labels this is an assumption. The gradient (WikiText 0% to web crawl 15-20%) could also reflect stylistic differences (template content, SEO text) unrelated to recursive generation. The paper acknowledges this but treats the gradient as confirmatory evidence.
76	
77	8. **No systematic error analysis.** Beyond per-class recall and per-topic accuracy (Table 4, prompted generation only), there is no analysis of what text characteristics predict misclassification. Text length, domain, lexical complexity, etc. would help understand failure modes.
78	
79	### Questions for Authors
80	1. How many passages per depth per model-strategy configuration? Total dataset size?
81	2. What is the performance of a simple MLP or fine-tuned LM probe on this task?
82	3. Has any reference model other than Pythia-1.4B been tested? How sensitive are results to reference model choice?
83	4. What is the computational cost of the full 28-feature extraction pipeline per passage?
84	5. How was the pre/post-2023 timestamp determined in the RedPajama temporal analysis? Are there content-type confounds (e.g., increased SEO content)?
85	
86	---
87	
88	## R2 Review: Novelty & Related Work
89	
90	### Strengths
91	
92	1. **Genuinely novel task formulation.** No prior work formalizes the problem of estimating recursive generation depth from text alone. The gap identification is clean: model collapse studies know depth (they control the pipeline), binary detectors discard depth information. Ordinal depth estimation fills a real methodological hole.
93	
94	2. **The Depth Degradation Atlas is a valuable empirical contribution type.** The systematic mapping of feature behavior across models, depths, and strategies, revealing non-obvious patterns (trajectory reversal, universal markers), aligns well with EMNLP's emphasis on empirical methods. This is not "just experiments" but a structured empirical investigation that produces reusable findings.
95	
96	3. **Strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal is a genuinely surprising finding.** Temperature sampling reversing feature trajectories at the d1/d2 boundary, causing naive cross-strategy AUC to drop to 0.13 (worse than random), is non-trivial. The mechanistic explanation (uniform logit scaling accelerates convergence to attractor distribution) is plausible and connects to Holtzman et al.'s observations about decoding strategy effects.
97	
98	4. **Strong narrative structure.** The paper follows a clear arc: problem definition, benchmark construction, Atlas findings, practical pipeline, real-world calibration. Each section builds on the previous. Contributions are clearly stated and each is supported by specific sections and tables. The Introduction effectively motivates why ordinal depth is more useful than binary labels.
99	
100	5. **Positioning against binary detectors is effective.** The argument that first-generation synthetic data may be useful (cite Gunasekar et al.) while deeply recursive text is degraded provides a compelling reason to move beyond binary classification to ordinal estimation.
101	
102	6. **Limitations are honest and thorough.** No over-claiming. Five specific limitations are described with appropriate caveats. This builds trust in the empirical findings.
103	
104	### Weaknesses
105	
106	1. **Methods are not novel.** RF/LR on 19 hand-crafted statistical features is a straightforward application of standard tools. The novelty is entirely in the problem formulation and empirical findings, not in algorithmic contribution. For EMNLP Main, reviewers may expect methodological innovation alongside empirical contribution.
107	
108	2. **Related Work is somewhat thin.**
109	   - ~22 references is below typical EMNLP density for a paper spanning model collapse, text detection, decoding effects, attribution, data curation, and watermarking. Papers in this space typically cite 30-40 works.
110	   - Missing: RAID benchmark (Dugan et al., 2024) for machine text detection evaluation; multi-class/multi-source detection methods; LLM-generated text quality evaluation frameworks; more recent 2025 model collapse works.
111	   - No discussion of how depth estimation relates to text quality scoring beyond the brief mention of QuRating/DSIR.
112	
113	3. **Section 6 "Atlas Visualizations" is vestigial.** A single sentence ("We present four key visualizations that constitute the empirical core of the Depth Degradation Atlas") followed by figures. This section should be integrated into Section 4 with per-figure analysis, or expanded with substantive discussion. As written, it reads like a placeholder.
114	
115	4. **Two-stage pipeline is obvious engineering.** Given that cross-strategy transfer catastrophically fails, "identify strategy first, then route to strategy-specific classifier" is the natural and expected response. The contribution is in execution and demonstration, not in conceptual innovation.
116	
117	5. **Gap between experimental setup and motivating scenario.** The paper motivates ordinal depth estimation for web-crawled training data contamination, where frontier models (GPT-4, Claude, etc.) generate text via API calls. But experiments use LoRA fine-tuning on small models (1-14B). Real-world recursive contamination likely involves heterogeneous models, strategies, and human editing at each depth. The prompted generation experiment (Section 5.3) partially bridges this gap but does not close it.
118	
119	6. **Minor writing issues.**
120	   - Abstract last sentence ("confirming ecological relevance and scale robustness") reads like a conclusion stamp rather than a result.
121	   - Section 4.2 reports multiple p-values inline without multiple testing correction.
122	   - Using "Atlas" as a proper noun throughout feels slightly self-aggrandizing for a systematic empirical study.
123	   - Some sentences are dense with inline metrics, reducing readability (e.g., Section 5.2 paragraph listing all AUC values and CIs).
124	
125	7. **Practical impact is uncertain.** The 15-20% FPR on web data, combined with ~58% d2 recall, means the tool would produce many false positives and miss many intermediate-depth texts. The paper proposes ensemble voting as mitigation but doesn't test it.
126	
127	### Questions for Authors
128	1. How does the approach handle mixed-depth text (e.g., human text revised by an LLM, or LLM text with human edits)?
129	2. Is there a plan to release the benchmark dataset and code for community use?
130	3. How would the approach generalize to non-English text, or to text from frontier-scale models (70B+)?
131	4. Could the Atlas be made extensible (e.g., a framework/toolkit for adding new models/strategies)?
132	5. What is the authors' view on the gap between the LoRA experimental setup and API-based text generation in the wild?
133	
134	---
135	
136	## Roundtable Step 2: Cross-Sharing Summary
137	
138	**R1 to R2:** Key experimental concerns are (1) d2 recall critically low for practical use, (2) sample sizes unreported, (3) no neural baselines to determine if 19 features suffice, (4) wild-data FPR estimates rest on untested assumptions. The methodology is sound but narrow in scope.
139	
140	**R2 to R1:** Key positioning concerns are (1) methods are standard, novelty is purely task/empirical, (2) Section 6 is vestigial, (3) ~22 references is thin for this breadth, (4) gap between experimental setup (LoRA, ≤14B) and motivating scenario (frontier model web contamination) is large. Writing quality is generally good but has minor issues.
141	
142	## Roundtable Step 3: Convergence
143	
144	Both reviewers converge on these points:
145	
146	**Agreements:**
147	- Task novelty is the paper's strongest asset. Ordinal depth estimation is genuinely new, well-motivated, and fills a real gap between model collapse studies and binary detectors.
148	- Experimental comprehensiveness is impressive for a first paper on a new task. The 3x3x4 benchmark + 14B + prompted + wild-data + two-stage pipeline represents substantial work.
149	- The Atlas findings (feature universality, monotonic AUC decay, trajectory reversal) are scientifically interesting and reusable.
150	- Standard methods (RF/LR) are the weakest dimension. For EMNLP Main, stronger methodological contribution is expected.
151	- Scope limitations (model scale, LoRA-only, English-only) are appropriate for a first paper but constrain significance claims.
152	- Practical utility remains unproven given d2 recall and FPR issues.
153	
154	**Disposition consensus:** The paper is a solid empirical contribution that opens a new task. It is above the bar for EMNLP Findings but borderline for Main conference. The gap to Main acceptance could be closed by: (1) adding neural classifier baselines, (2) demonstrating practical utility in a downstream curation task, or (3) extending to frontier-scale models.
155	
156	## Roundtable Step 4: Consolidated Assessment
157	
158	### Overall Verdict: Borderline Accept (3/5)
159	
160	This paper makes a genuine contribution by formalizing ordinal generation depth estimation as a new task and providing the first comprehensive empirical investigation (the Depth Degradation Atlas). The task is novel, timely, and well-motivated by the growing concern over recursive synthetic contamination in training data.
161	
162	**What pushes toward acceptance:**
163	- Novel task with clear gap identification
164	- Comprehensive Atlas covering scales, strategies, and generation modes
165	- Non-obvious finding (temperature trajectory reversal) with mechanistic explanation
166	- Thorough limitations section
167	- 100% coverage of planned experiments
168	
169	**What pushes toward rejection:**
170	- Standard methods (RF/LR) with no neural baselines
171	- Limited model scale (max 14B, single data point)
172	- Low d2 recall undermines practical utility claims
173	- Thin related work (~22 references)
174	- Gap between experimental setup and real-world motivating scenario
175	
176	**Recommendation for authors:**
177	1. **Priority 1:** Add at least one neural baseline (MLP on features, or fine-tuned LM probe) to determine if the feature bottleneck explains low d2 recall
178	2. **Priority 2:** Report sample sizes clearly and add a held-out test evaluation
179	3. **Priority 3:** Expand Related Work to ~30-35 references, including RAID, multi-source detection, and 2025 model collapse works
180	4. **Priority 4:** Merge Section 6 into Section 4 with per-figure analysis
181	5. **Priority 5:** If feasible, add one 70B experiment to strengthen scale claims
182	
183	### Final Scores
184	
185	| Dimension | Score | Justification |
186	|-----------|-------|---------------|
187	| Soundness | **4** (Strong) | Experimental methodology is rigorous within its scope. Statistical analysis is appropriate (CV, CIs, AUC, KS tests). Limitations are honestly discussed. The main concern is scope (model scale, classifier type) rather than correctness. |
188	| Excitement | **3** (Good) | Task is novel and timely. Atlas findings (especially trajectory reversal) are interesting. But methods are standard, model scale is limited, and practical impact is uncertain given d2 recall and FPR issues. |
189	| Overall | **3** (Good) | Solid empirical paper that opens a new task. Appropriate for EMNLP Findings. Borderline for Main. The paper defines the problem well and establishes baselines, but lacks the methodological innovation or demonstrated practical impact typically expected for Main conference acceptance. |
190	
191	**Confidence:** 4/5 (fairly confident, but acknowledge that task novelty could justify Main acceptance if area chairs value problem formulation contributions).
```

The recursive generation chain formulation is unambiguous and creates a clear experimental protocol. The choice of diverse model families (Pythia, OLMo, GPT-2 XL) with different architectures and training data strengthens generalizability claims. Extension to d=5 for limit analysis is appropriate.\n\n3. **Fixed reference model design.** Using Pythia-1.4B as a fixed reference across all experiments is a key methodological insight. It ensures features reflect text properties rather than generator-reference alignment, and enables cross-model comparability. The 14B experiment validates this: Pythia-1.4B retains discriminative power on 14B-generated text with only 2-4pp degradation.\n\n4. **Feature interpretability.** 19 features in 4 families (perplexity, surprisal, diversity, fluency) are transparent, reproducible, and computationally cheap. The finding that p90_ppl and var_surprisal are universal top-2 features across all 9 configurations, at 14B scale, and under prompted generation is a robust empirical result.\n\n5. **Scale validation addresses the right concern.** Using Qwen2.5-14B in clean FP16 (no quantization) directly resolves the 7B QLoRA confound (U-shaped curves). The result (76.2%, within 1B range) falsifies the \"small model artifact\" hypothesis. Identifying the U-shape as quantization noise is a useful methodological finding.\n\n6. **Ecological validity is statistically supported.** The prompted generation experiment (50 prompts, 5 domain clusters, 92.8% accuracy retention) with KS tests confirming feature distribution stability (p > 0.05 for all top-5 features) provides solid evidence. Cross-topic variance is small (std = 3.1pp), ruling out content domain as a confound.\n\n7. **Two-stage pipeline shows systematic problem-solving.** The v1 (57.5% strategy clf) to v2 (81.2%) progression with principled feature additions targeting strategy-discriminative signals is well-motivated. The oracle analysis (78.4% ≈ within-strategy 78.2%, gap = 0.2pp) confirms the architecture ceiling and localizes remaining error to strategy misclassification.\n\n8. **Honest limitations.** Five specific limitations clearly described. Wild-data temporal analysis appropriately caveated (\"does not constitute causal evidence\"). For 5-fold stratified CV, total N per class directly affects CI width and the reliability of the reported accuracies. The 14B confusion matrix (Figure 3) shows d2 recall of 0.58 with 25% of d2 classified as d3. For practical data curation, graduated decisions at middle depths are exactly the advertised use case. This undermines the practical contribution.\n\n4. **Single 14B data point for \"scale robustness.\"** The claim that the signal persists \"at the 14B scale\" rests on one experiment with one model (Qwen2.5-14B) and one strategy (nucleus). 8	# Dream Review Report: "How Deep Is This Text? Ordinal Depth Estimation for Recursively Generated Content"
9	
10	**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 Main
11	**Review Mode**: Full (2 Reviewers + Roundtable)
12	**Dream ID**: 20260517T075852
13	
14	---
15	
16	## Converged Scores
17	
18	| Dimension | R1 (Methods+Exp) | R2 (Novelty+RW) | Converged |
19	|-----------|:-:|:-:|:-:|
20	| Soundness | 3.0 | 3.5 | 3.0 |
21	| Excitement | 3.5 | 3.0 | 3.0 |
22	| Overall | 3.0 | 3.0 | **3.0** (Findings accept / borderline reject) |
23	| Confidence | 4 | 4 | 4 |
24	
25	**Headline**: Borderline. Well-motivated new task with thorough controlled experiments, but technical novelty of the classifier pipeline is limited, key baselines against existing detectors are missing, and the gap between controlled and realistic settings undermines practical claims. Findings-track viable; Main conference acceptance unlikely without revisions.
26	
27	---
28	
29	## 1. Paper Summary
30	
31	The paper formalizes *ordinal generation depth estimation* as a new task: given text alone, predict how many recursive fine-tuning cycles it has undergone (depth 0-3). Using 19 reference-model perplexity features and Random Forest classifiers, the authors build a 3-model x 3-strategy benchmark at 1-1.5B scale (70-85% four-class accuracy), validate signal persistence at 14B (67%, ~13pp attenuation) and under prompted generation (67%, 80% retention), and construct a Depth Degradation Atlas documenting universal depth markers, non-uniform inter-depth distances, scale-dependent EDD, and strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversals. A cross-strategy two-stage pipeline validates the decomposition principle (oracle 78.4% ≈ within-strategy 78.2%) but identifies strategy classification (57.5%) as the practical bottleneck, with naive pooling (72.0%) as a strong zero-cost baseline.
32	
33	---
34	
35	## 2. Reviewer 1 (R1): Methods + Experiments
36	
37	### Strengths
38	1. **Novel, well-defined task**: Ordinal depth estimation is a genuinely new problem formulation that fills a real gap between binary detection and model collapse characterization.
39	2. **Systematic benchmark**: The 3-model x 3-strategy x 4-depth grid is comprehensive at the 1B scale, and the paper honestly reports both successes and limitations.
40	3. **Useful concepts**: The EDD metric is cleanly formalized and the oracle-vs-within decomposition (78.4% ≈ 78.2%) is an elegant validation of the two-stage principle.
41	4. **Honest negative results**: The DCOL failure (Section 5.5) and the two-stage pipeline underperforming naive pooling are reported transparently, increasing credibility.
42	5. **Multiple robustness axes**: Scale (14B), generation mode (prompted), and cross-strategy transfer are all examined.
43	
44	### Weaknesses
45	1. **(Major) Incomplete statistical reporting**: The core 1B benchmark (Table 1) lacks confidence intervals and no significance tests appear for systematic cross-condition comparisons. The 14B and prompted experiments do report CIs, creating an inconsistency. For a paper making quantitative claims about ~13pp attenuation and 80% retention, bootstrap tests or at minimum CIs on all main results are expected.
46	2. **(Major) No fine-tuning sensitivity analysis**: The entire depth signal is produced by LoRA fine-tuning with one configuration (r=16, alpha=32, lr=2e-4, 3 epochs). The paper includes a LoRA rank ablation (r=4 to 64, 4.65pp spread) and cross-seed replication (delta=1.05pp), which partially addresses this. However, the interaction between learning rate, epoch count, and depth signal magnitude remains unexplored. If a different fine-tuning recipe produces substantially different depth signals, the benchmark's generality is in question.
47	3. **(Major) Scale validation is thin**: The 14B experiment uses one model (Qwen2.5-14B) under one strategy (nucleus). "Scale-dependent attenuation" as a general claim requires more than one data point. The paper mentions preliminary 7B results with acknowledged confounds, but these are insufficient.
48	4. **(Minor) Single reference model**: All features depend on Pythia-410M. While the paper mentions an appendix ablation, the main text does not discuss sensitivity to reference model choice.
49	5. **(Minor) Narrow prompted generation evaluation**: 10 topic prompts on 1 model (GPT-2 XL) is a limited test of ecological validity. Prompt diversity, length, and style could all affect the 80% retention figure.
50	6. **(Minor) Cross-strategy d1-d3 accuracy is near-chance**: Excluding the trivially separable d0, d1-d3 cross-strategy accuracy drops to ~45.9%, barely above 33% chance. This is underemphasized relative to the practical deployment framing.
51	
52	### Questions for Authors
53	1. Can you provide bootstrap CIs for all entries in Table 1?
54	2. What happens to depth estimation accuracy when LoRA learning rate or epoch count is varied by 2x?
55	3. Have you tried Pythia-160M or GPT-2 Small as alternative reference models?
56	4. For the prompted experiment, what is the variance across the 10 topic prompts?
57	
58	---
59	
60	## 3. Reviewer 2 (R2): Novelty + Related Work
61	
62	### Strengths
63	1. **Task formalization is the core contribution**: Moving from "is this text AI-generated?" to "how many recursive generations deep is this text?" is a meaningful conceptual advance with clear practical motivation for data curation.
64	2. **Strategy-dependent trajectory reversal is genuinely surprising**: The finding that temperature sampling reverses the d1-d2 feature boundary (AUC as low as 0.13) is non-obvious and has real implications for detector deployment.
65	3. **Well-calibrated claims**: The paper does not overclaim. "Signal persists with attenuation" is more honest and informative than "robust across scales." The limitations section is thorough.
66	4. **Clean paper structure**: The narrative flows logically from task definition through atlas construction to robustness validation and deployment analysis.
67	
68	### Weaknesses
69	1. **(Major) No comparison with existing detectors repurposed for ordinal estimation**: The paper positions against Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT in the introduction but never tests whether their confidence scores, when recalibrated into ordinal bins, can estimate depth. This is the most critical missing experiment. Without it, the paper cannot establish that the task genuinely requires new methods rather than trivial recalibration of existing ones. Even a negative result (showing existing detectors fail at ordinal estimation) would strengthen the paper substantially.
70	2. **(Major) The Depth Degradation Atlas overpromises**: The name suggests a systematic theoretical framework, but the actual content is a collection of empirical observations (universal features, non-uniform distances, EDD, trajectory reversal) without unifying theory. Each observation is valuable individually, but packaging them as an "Atlas" implies more conceptual coherence than is delivered.
71	3. **(Major) Gap between controlled and realistic settings**: Real-world recursive contamination involves pretraining on mixed corpora at unknown proportions, not sequential LoRA fine-tuning on pure depth-k data. The 20-22% false positive rate on web-crawl data (mentioned in Limitations) and the cross-strategy fragility (59.1% LOO) suggest the controlled results may not transfer. The paper acknowledges this but the abstract and introduction frame the method as more broadly applicable than the evidence supports.
72	4. **(Minor) Related Work is thin**: 22 references is light for EMNLP. Missing: DetectGPT (Mitchell et al., 2023) is cited but DNA-GPT, RADAR, and other recent detection methods are absent. Model collapse mitigation work (e.g., Gerstgrasser et al.'s data mixing strategies) is mentioned but not discussed in depth. The Related Work reads more as a citation list than a positioning argument.
73	5. **(Minor) Zero figures in the paper**: The paper references figure environments but no actual figures appear in the PDF. For a paper whose core contribution is a "Depth Degradation Atlas," visual presentation is critical. Heatmaps, feature trajectory plots, and AUC decay curves would substantially improve comprehension and impact. (R1 notes this may be a compilation artifact, but the submitted PDF lacks them.)
74	6. **(Minor) Technical novelty of the pipeline is limited**: RF on 19 hand-crafted perplexity features is a straightforward approach. The features themselves (mean/std/percentiles of perplexity and surprisal) are standard statistical summaries. The contribution is in the task definition and empirical findings, not in methodological innovation.
75	
76	### Questions for Authors
77	1. What happens when you threshold Binoculars or Fast-DetectGPT scores into 4 ordinal bins? Even a simple experiment would clarify whether existing detectors already solve the task.
78	2. Can you provide a theoretical or intuitive argument for why the Atlas observations should be expected to hold beyond LoRA fine-tuning on small models?
79	3. The 20% FPR on web-crawl data: does this mean 1 in 5 human-written web texts would be classified as depth-1+? How does this affect the practical data curation application?
80	4. Why are there no figures in the submitted PDF?
81	
82	---
83	
84	## 4. Roundtable Discussion Summary
85	
86	### Points of Convergence
87	- **Both reviewers agree on Overall 3.0**: The paper defines a well-motivated new task and executes thorough experiments within a controlled scope, but falls short of Main conference acceptance due to limited technical novelty, missing baselines, and insufficient evidence for generalization beyond the controlled setting.
88	- **Missing existing-detector comparison is the single most actionable gap**: Both reviewers agree this is the highest-priority revision. Even showing existing detectors fail at ordinal estimation would substantially strengthen the contribution.
89	- **Scale validation is insufficient for generality claims**: One 14B model under one strategy does not support "scale-dependent" as a general phenomenon. Both reviewers agree additional scale points or qualifying the claim would help.
90	- **Statistical reporting needs strengthening**: CIs on all main results and significance tests for key comparisons are expected at EMNLP.
91	- **Cross-strategy fragility is underemphasized**: The 59.1% LOO (45.9% excluding d0) fundamentally limits practical deployment, yet the framing is more optimistic than the evidence warrants.
92	
93	### Points of Disagreement
94	- **Missing LoRA hyperparameters (R1 W1)**: R2 found that hyperparameters are actually specified in Section 3.2 (r=16, alpha=32, lr=2e-4, 3 epochs). R1 may have missed this or the detail may be insufficiently prominent. Downgraded from major to minor after discussion.
95	- **Zero figures (R2 W5)**: R1 noted that LaTeX source contains figure environments with captions, suggesting a compilation artifact rather than deliberate omission. Kept as minor since the submitted PDF lacks them regardless.
96	- **Excitement weighting**: R1 finds the task formalization and Atlas more exciting (3.5) while R2 is more reserved (3.0), viewing the RF pipeline as technically lightweight. Converged to 3.0, reflecting that excitement for the task is tempered by limited methodological novelty.
97	
98	### Newly Raised During Discussion
99	- **Cross-strategy d1-d3 near-chance performance**: R1 emphasized that excluding d0, cross-strategy accuracy is essentially near-chance (45.9% vs 33%). This is a serious practical limitation that both reviewers agree is underemphasized.
100	- **Ecological validity gap is more fundamental than hyperparameter gaps**: R2 argued that the distance between controlled LoRA fine-tuning and real-world recursive contamination (mixed corpora, unknown procedures, heterogeneous models) is the paper's deepest limitation, more important than any specific experimental detail.
101	
102	---
103	
104	## 5. Experiment Coverage Assessment
105	
106	Comparing the paper against the experiment design reference (review_context.yaml):
107	
108	| Experiment | Status in Paper | Assessment |
109	|---|---|---|
110	| 1B 3x3 Benchmark | Fully covered (Table 1) | Complete |
111	| DCOL Ablation | Cancelled (correctly); negative result reported (Sec 5.5) | Appropriate |
112	| 14B Scale Validation (plan_014) | Covered (Sec 5.2, Tables 2-5) | Present but thin (1 model, 1 strategy) |
113	| Wild-data Calibration (plan_015) | Deferred to AAAI; mentioned in Limitations | Acceptable deferral |
114	| Prompted Generation (plan_016) | Covered (Sec 5.3, Tables 6-8) | Present but narrow (1 model, 10 prompts) |
115	| Cross-Strategy Pipeline (plan_017) | Fully covered (Sec 5.4, Tables 9-11) | Complete and well-analyzed |
116	
117	**Coverage**: 4 of 5 non-cancelled experiments are covered. The wild-data deferral is acceptable given the paper's scope. No critical experiments are missing from the design, though depth of coverage varies.
118	
119	---
120	
121	## 6. Actionable Recommendations (Priority-Ordered)
122	
123	### Must-Fix for Main Conference Resubmission
124	1. **Add existing detector comparison**: Run Binoculars and/or Fast-DetectGPT on the benchmark, threshold scores into ordinal bins, report accuracy. Even a negative result is highly valuable.
125	2. **Strengthen scale validation**: Add at least one more model family at 7B+ scale (e.g., Llama-3-8B or Mistral-7B) under nucleus sampling. Alternatively, qualify "scale-dependent" claims to acknowledge the single-model limitation.
126	3. **Complete statistical reporting**: Add bootstrap CIs to all Table 1 entries. Add significance tests (paired bootstrap or McNemar's) for key comparisons (1B vs 14B, BOS vs prompted, within vs pooled).
127	4. **Fix figure compilation**: Ensure all referenced figures appear in the submitted PDF. The Atlas contribution depends heavily on visual presentation.
128	
129	### Should-Fix
130	5. **Add fine-tuning sensitivity**: Vary lr by 2x and epoch count (1, 3, 5) to show depth signal robustness to fine-tuning recipe.
131	6. **Expand prompted evaluation**: Test on at least one additional model beyond GPT-2 XL; vary prompt diversity.
132	7. **Strengthen Related Work**: Add 8-10 references covering recent detection methods (DNA-GPT, RADAR), model collapse mitigation strategies, and data contamination detection work.
133	8. **Recalibrate practical claims**: The abstract and introduction should more clearly scope the contribution to controlled settings, acknowledging cross-strategy fragility and web-crawl FPR.
134	
135	### Nice-to-Have
136	9. **Reference model ablation in main text**: Briefly discuss sensitivity to reference model choice.
137	10. **Unify Atlas framing**: Either provide a more cohesive theoretical framework connecting the four Atlas dimensions, or rename it to avoid overpromising.
138	
139	---
140	
141	## 7. Overall Assessment
142	
143	This paper introduces a genuinely useful new task (ordinal generation depth estimation) and provides the first systematic empirical investigation across models, strategies, and scales. The Depth Degradation Atlas contains several interesting findings, particularly the strategy-dependent trajectory reversal and the scale-dependent EDD concept. The honest reporting of negative results and signal attenuation builds scientific credibility.
144	
145	However, the paper falls short of EMNLP Main acceptance for three reasons: (1) the absence of existing detector baselines leaves the necessity of the proposed approach unestablished; (2) the technical pipeline (RF on perplexity features) is methodologically lightweight; and (3) the gap between controlled experiments and real-world applicability (20% web-crawl FPR, 59.1% cross-strategy LOO, single-model scale validation) limits the practical impact claimed in the framing.
146	
147	The paper is well-suited for **EMNLP Findings** in its current form and could reach **Main conference** with the must-fix revisions above, particularly the existing detector comparison and strengthened statistical reporting.
148	
149	**Converged Overall Score: 3.0 (Findings acceptance)**
150

The Atlas contribution depends heavily on visual presentation.\n\n### Should-Fix\n5. **Add fine-tuning sensitivity**: Vary lr by 2x and epoch count (1, 3, 5) to show depth signal robustness to fine-tuning recipe.\n6. **Expand prompted evaluation**: Test on at least one additional model beyond GPT-2 XL; vary prompt diversity.\n7. **Strengthen Related Work**: Add 8-10 references covering recent detection methods (DNA-GPT, RADAR), model collapse mitigation strategies, and data contamination detection work.\n8. **Recalibrate practical claims**: The abstract and introduction should more clearly scope the contribution to controlled settings, acknowledging cross-strategy fragility and web-crawl FPR.\n\n### Nice-to-Have\n9. **Reference model ablation in main text**: Briefly discuss sensitivity to reference model choice.\n10. **Unify Atlas framing**: Either provide a more cohesive theoretical framework connecting the four Atlas dimensions, or rename it to avoid overpromising.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Overall Assessment\n\nThis paper introduces a genuinely useful new task (ordinal generation depth estimation) and provides the first systematic empirical investigation across models, strategies, and scales. The Depth Degradation Atlas contains several interesting findings, particularly the strategy-dependent trajectory reversal and the scale-dependent EDD concept. The honest reporting of negative results and signal attenuation builds scientific credibility.\n\nHowever, the paper falls short of EMNLP Main acceptance for three reasons: (1) the absence of existing detector baselines leaves the necessity of the proposed approach unestablished; (2) the technical pipeline (RF on perplexity features) is methodologically lightweight; and (3) the gap between controlled experiments and real-world applicability (20% web-crawl FPR, 59.1% cross-strategy LOO, single-model scale validation) limits the practical impact claimed in the framing.\n\nThe paper is well-suited for **EMNLP Findings** in its current form and could reach **Main conference** with the must-fix revisions above, particularly the existing detector comparison and strengthened statistical reporting.\n\n**Converged Overall Score: 3.0 (Findings acceptance)**\n",

```text
Now I have a comprehensive picture. Let me analyze the Dream results and the Manager's self-audit directive together.

## Dream Results Summary

**Best case**: Borderline Accept (3/3/3) — "Borderline accept for EMNLP Main. Strong candidate for Findings."
**Median case**: Findings acceptance / borderline reject (3.0) — "Findings-track viable; Main conference acceptance unlikely without revisions."
**Worst case**: Borderline reject (2.5) — "Borderline Findings / Resubmit."

### Cross-tier Issues (structural, appear in all 3):


Every reviewer tier flags the same core problems: missing calibrated detector baselines like Binoculars and adapted Fast-DetectGPT, limited methodological novelty beyond random forests on standard features, inflated d0 metrics, insufficient references (22 vs. 30+), fragility across different strategies, and missing confidence intervals on the main results table. The worst-case scenario adds a much more critical assessment of ecological validity to these shared concerns.

I'm realizing that my worst-case projections—EDD collapsing to 55% accuracy on 14B models and 50% on prompted generation—are hypothetical numbers, not actual experimental results. My real experiments (plan_014 with 13B and plan_016 with prompted generation) are still in progress, and the paper framing document actually has more optimistic expectations based on preliminary data I've already collected.

Now I need to step back and do the comprehensive self-audit the manager requested: reviewing what experiments I've actually completed, identifying which standard baselines are still missing, determining if I need more experiments, and honestly assessing the scope of the work.

Looking at my experiment plan, I've completed 19 out of 26 experiments (73%), with 2 currently running—qwen25_14b_scale_001 and prompted_gen_gpt2xl_001—though these registry IDs don't match what's listed in my plan file yet. I'm noticing a discrepancy where plan_014 and plan_016 show as "planned" in the plan file but are actually running in the registry, and they're missing their registry_id mappings, which is something I need to fix.

Beyond the sync issue, I'm realizing there's a critical gap in my baselines: I haven't adapted Binoculars or Fast-DetectGPT for ordinal depth scoring, even though both could produce continuous scores that I could threshold into ordinal bins. This would be a valuable addition to strengthen the paper, and I should prioritize getting this baseline in place.

Looking at what I've actually run, plan_009 covered DivEye, perplexity regression, CORAL, and CE, but I'm missing those detector baselines entirely. I also notice the neural baseline situation is a bit muddled—I have DeBERTa from plan_008 achieving 58.3%, but it's end-to-end rather than operating on my 19 features, which actually makes it useful as a comparison point showing that random forests on features outperform the neural approach.

For scope, I'm working with three model families at 1-1.5B scale (Pythia, OLMo, GPT-2 XL) plus Qwen2.5 at 14B that's currently running, and I've got three generation strategies covered (nucleus, temperature, top-k).

I've got depths d0-d3 as my main focus with extensions to d0-d5 for EDD, both BOS-seeded and prompted generation modes, a solid set of baselines including RF, LR, CORAL, DivEye, CE-MLP, perplexity regression, and DeBERTa, plus cross-generalization tests across models and strategies with seed transfer, and ablations on granularity, generation length, and LoRA rank. What's still blocking me for the main conference submission is adding Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT (a 2-4 hour CPU/GPU task), expanding my references from 22 to 30+, computing confidence intervals for Table 1, and finishing the MLP analysis.

For immediate next steps, I'm prioritizing the detector baselines since that's the biggest gap, then computing CIs for Table 1 and updating my experiment registry. I've got two plans currently running—plan_014 on cuda:0 downloading the 13B FP16 model and plan_016 on cuda:1 working through prompted generation at d2. Once those finish, I'll write up the results in the appendix, adjust the paper framing based on what I find, and get it resubmitted. I also need to handle the manager's directive and look into the dream report.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The dream-report-action skill file doesn't exist. Let me proceed with the analysis directly based on the CLAUDE.md instructions about dream reports:

"**收到 `[DREAM {id}] 完成` 后**：按注入消息中的行动要求逐步执行，禁止"已知悉"式跳过。"

And from the skill description: "Dream 完成后的审稿反馈分析协议。区分通用建议与数据依赖建议， 跨三档对比验证有效性，构建行动列表并持久化。"


Now I'm analyzing the cross-tier feedback to identify structural issues that all three tiers agree on. The missing calibrated detector baselines—specifically Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT—emerge as the highest priority gap, flagged consistently across all tiers as the single most actionable experimental addition, and I should add this to the plan and assign it. The references also need strengthening, currently at around 22 citations when the paper should have 30+ to adequately cover the DNA-GP literature.

Beyond that, there's the issue of d0 inflation in the headline metrics—we're already reporting the 3-class results in tables but need to reframe the Abstract and Introduction to lead with d1-d3 results or provide clearer context. The confidence intervals on Table 1 are missing but computable from our 5-fold cross-validation results, so that's a straightforward addition. Section 6 on Atlas Visualizations feels redundant and should be merged into Section 4 with per-figure analysis. Finally, the cross-strategy fragility deserves more emphasis relative to our practical claims, which all three tiers flagged as underexplored.

The two-stage pipeline results from plan_017 are already in hand, so this is mainly a framing adjustment. For the 14B single data point, the severity depends on what our experiment actually yields—if we get above 70% accuracy, we can honestly address the single-model concern, but if it lands around 60%, we'll need to discuss scale-dependent attenuation and acknowledge the limitation. Similarly, the prompted generation narrowness concern hinges on whether we achieve strong accuracy retention or face significant degradation.

The ecological validity gap is the most concerning in worst-case scenarios, but again, better actual results would mitigate this. For now, I'm waiting on the experiment outcomes to determine how to frame these issues. Once I have the data, I can adjust the narrative accordingly.

On the structural side, there are several must-do items: adding a Binoculars/Fast-DetectGPT ordinal baseline experiment, computing confidence intervals for all Table 1 entries using bootstrap from cross-validation, expanding the reference list to 30+, adjusting the Abstract and Introduction to properly contextualize the d0 inflation issue, and merging Section 6 into Section 4.

After the experiments run, I'll write up the 13B and prompted generation results in the appendix with brief mentions in the main text, then adjust the framing based on what we actually find. There are also some nice-to-have additions like an MLP baseline on 19 features and an alternative reference model ablation, though those are lower priority.

The manager also asked me to audit what experiments I've already run, check against standard baselines in the field to catch any gaps, and evaluate whether additional experiments are needed—I'm working through that checklist now. 要补充实验以增强论文说服力
4. 审视当前研究 scope 是否偏小，是否需要扩展

Let me combine the Dream analysis with the Manager's audit request.

### Manager Self-Audit Response:

**1. 实验覆盖情况**
- 26 个计划实验中 19 完成（73.1%），2 运行中（13B FP16 + prompted gen），5 未开始（1 取消，1 推迟，3 待启动）
- 跨 claim 覆盖：Claim 1 (78.9%), Claim 2 (80.0%), Claim 3 (0% plan完成但 registry 已有结果)
- 额外完成了 plan 外的约 64 个实验，涵盖种子复现、长度消融和跨策略管道等

**2. 缺失 baseline**
- ⚠️ **Binoculars / Fast-DetectGPT 作为 ordinal baseline** — Dream 三档一致认为这是最关键遗漏，这两个是当前领域最主流的 AI 文本检测器，产出连续分数，需要将分数校准为 ordinal bin 后作为 baseline 来验证深度估计能力，即使结果为负也能大幅加强论文立论
- ⚠️ **MLP on 19 features** — 简单神经网络 baseline，用来验证

特征瓶颈是否限制了 d2 recall

**3. 需要补充的实验**
- Binoculars/Fast-DetectGPT ordinal baseline（CPU/GPU 混合，2-4h）
- MLP on 19 features（CPU only，<1h）
- Table 1 bootstrap CIs（CPU only，<1h）

**4. Scope 评估**
- 模型覆盖：3 families × 1-1.5B + 1 × 14B = 合理但不突出
- 策略覆盖：3 种 = 领域标准
- 深度：d0-d5 = 充分
- **关键短板**：没跑过 Binoculars/Fast-DetectGPT，这在 AI 文本检测领域是公认标准，是论文的重大遗漏

整体来看，scope 虽然充足但不奢华，关键是补齐这些 baseline 实验。我需要先给用户发送综合分析回复，然后指示 Agent 运行这些 baseline。但首先要检查实验计划文件，同步 registry_ids，因为计划中的 plan_014/016/017 标记为"planned"，但实际的实验在 registry 中用了不同的 ID 并且已经在运行或完成。

{
  "text": "## 自查报告 + Dream 审稿分析\n\n### 一、Dream 审稿结果（dream_20260517T075852）\n\n| 档位 | 总分 | 定位 |\n|------|------|------|\n| Best | 3/5 | Borderline Accept Main，强 Findings |\n| Median | 3.0 | Findings accept / borderline reject |\n| Worst | 2.5 | Borderline Findings / resubmit |\n\n**三档一致指出的结构性问题**（与实验结果无关，必须修）：\n\n1. ⭐ **缺少 Binoculars / Fast-DetectGPT 作为 ordinal baseline** — 三档都认为这是 #1 实验缺口。这两个检测器产出连续分数，校准为 ordinal bin 后的表现应作为 baseline。即使结果为负也大幅加强论文\n2. **引用不足**（~22 篇，需 30+）— 缺 DNA-GPT, RADAR, 多源归因, 数据过滤 pipeline 等\n3. **d0 膨胀 headline 指标** — Abstract \"70-85%\" 被 d0 near-perfect 抬高，需调整措辞或主推 d1-d3\n4. **Table 1 缺 CI** — 5-fold CV 结构支持 bootstrap CI，但未报告\n5. **RF/LR 方法论新颖性有限** — 三档都认为这是 Main vs Findings 的分水岭\n\n**数据依赖问题**（取决于运行中实验结果）：\n- Best 想象 14B 达 76.2%，Worst 想象仅 55% — 实际结果将决定 scale claim 强度\n- Best 想象 prompted gen 保留 92.8%，Worst 想象仅 50% — 同理\n\n### 二、实验覆盖自查\n\n**plan 内 26 个实验**：19 完成 + 2 运行中 + 1 取消 + 1 推迟 + 3 registry 已有但 plan 未更新\n\n**plan 外额外实验**（registry 共 90 个）：种子复现、长度消融、跨策略管道（已完成+11.5pp）、LoRA rank 消融等\n\n**按 Claim 覆盖率**：\n- Claim 1（深度估计可行）：78.9%，15/19 完成\n- Claim 2（Atlas/EDD）：80.0%，4/5 完成\n- Claim 3（跨策略管道 + downstream）：registry 有 cross_strategy_pipeline_001 完成，plan 未同步\n\n### 三、领域标准 baseline 遗漏检查\n\n| Baseline | 状态 | 优先级 |\n|----------|------|--------|\n| Binoculars 校准为 ordinal | ❌ 未做 | P0 必补 |\n| Fast-DetectGPT 校准为 ordinal | ❌ 未做 | P0 必补 |\n| RF/LR/CORAL/DivEye/CE-MLP | ✅ 已做 | — |\n| DeBERTa end-to-end | ✅ 已做（58.3%） | — |\n| PPL regression | ✅ 已做 | — |\n| MLP on 19 features | ❌ 未做 | P1 加分项 |\n\n**结论**：Binoculars/Fast-DetectGPT 是本领域公认的 SOTA 检测器，不跑它们作为 baseline 会被审稿人直接追问。Dream 三档一致确认这是最关键遗漏。\n\n### 四、Scope 评估\n\n当前 scope（3 模型族 × 3 策略 × 4-6 深度 + 14B scale + prompted gen + 跨策略管道）对于 task formalization + empirical study 论文已经**充足**。不需要扩展更多模型/策略/语言。\n\n**唯一需要补的是 baseline，不是 scope。**\n\n### 五、行动计划\n\n**立即派 Agent 做（不占 GPU 或少量 GPU）**：\n1. Binoculars + Fast-DetectGPT ordinal baseline（在已有 3×3 数据上跑，预计 2-4h）\n2. Table 1 所有 entry 的 bootstrap CI\n3. MLP on 19 features（CPU only）\n\n**继续等待运行中实验**：\n- 13B FP16（cuda:0，下载模型中，预计还需 ~18h）\n- Prompted gen（cuda:1，d2 stage，预计 2-4h）\n\n**论文写作侧（后续派 Paper Agent）**：\n- 引用扩充到 30+\n- 调整 Abstract/Intro d0 膨胀措辞\n- Section 6 合并到 Section 4\n\n现在派 Agent 执行 baseline 实验。"
